Source breakdown:\n\n| SmartGEP Account | Count | Notes |\n|-----------------|-------|-------|\n| dyna-segmen | 2,283 | Largest contributor |\n| dyna-om | 2,096 | |\n| consurv-technic | 842 | |\n| etimad (no entity) | 110 | Separate source |\n\nOf the 5,331, status distribution:\n\n| Status | Count | Pct |\n|--------|-------|-----|\n| draft | 2,576 | 48% |\n| closed | 2,399 | 45% |\n| insufficient_data | 118 | 2% |\n| awarded | 87 | 2% |\n| matched | 26 | <1% |\n| new | 15 | <1% |\n\nClosing dates span from **2020-09-09 to 2026-08-04** — this is historical backfill, not fresh tenders. 2,399 tenders (45%) were already past closing date on arrival and went straight to closed. The 87 awarded are historical records, not new wins.\n\n**Connection:**\n\nThe devops expert's learning that \"Bridge ingest runs as callback after each account completes\" explains the mechanism — a scraper configuration change or re-crawl of historical pages triggered bulk ingestion. The architect's learning about \"Reading actual data to understand the full data shape before designing\" applies here: the pipeline treated historical tenders identically to fresh ones, with no date-based filtering at ingestion.\n\nThe analyst's insight that \"emis.com is the dominant wrong-website source (24/44)\" shows data quality issues are a recurring theme — the pipeline lacks quality gates at the ingestion boundary.\n\n**Action items:**\n- [ ] **[CRITICAL]** Add ingestion-time guard: reject tenders where closing_date < today (or flag as `historical` status instead of `new`/`draft`)\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Audit the 2,576 draft tenders from Jun 30 — are any commercially viable, or is this noise?\n- [ ] **[MEDIUM]** Add daily ingestion rate alerting — flag when rate exceeds 3x average (>450/day)\n\n---\n\n### Insight 2: Etimad's Manual Match Collapsed — 46 Matched Reverted to 6, Proving Pipeline Can't Sustain It (HIGH)\n\n**Evidence:**\n\nW26 reported a promising etimad resurrection: 46 matched and 113 draft, up from zero. W27 reality:\n\n| Metric | W26 | W27 | Change |\n|--------|-----|-----|--------|\n| matched | 46 | 6 | -40 (87% loss) |\n| draft | 113 | 342 | +229 |\n| insufficient_data | 387 | 348 | -39 |\n| closed | 16,176 | 16,255 | +79 |\n| watching | 12 | 13 | +1 |\n\nThe 46 matched tenders (all stamped at the same `2026-06-27T03:53` timestamp) have largely been closed — confirming W26's suspicion that this was a one-off manual intervention. The draft growth from 113 to 342 (all 229 new drafts created since Jun 29) suggests an automated process is now generating drafts, but they're not progressing to matched.\n\n**Connection:**\n\nThis validates the reviewer's principle: \"Always verify builder's claims against actual code.\" The etimad \"resurrection\" was real data movement, but ephemeral. The same pattern appears in the conductor's learning: \"conductor announced builder was 'BUILDING' but never wrote the GOAL file\" — announcements of progress without durable state changes.\n\nW26's recommendation to \"identify what created the 46 matched\" is now moot — they reverted. The new question is: what's generating the 229 new drafts, and why can't they progress?\n\n**Action items:**\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Investigate the 342 etimad drafts — what blocks progression from draft to matched?\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Sample 5 etimad drafts vs 5 smartgep matched — compare field completeness to identify the matcher's blind spots\n- [ ] **[MEDIUM]** Add pipeline stage-transition monitoring — alert when tenders enter a status but never exit it within 7 days\n\n---\n\n### Insight 3: Builder Expert Has Zero Learnings — Corrupted Log Reveals Knowledge Drain (HIGH)\n\n**Evidence:**\n\nThe builder's `learnings.md` contains 21 duplicate retro entries — all variations of \"Build X\" (3 steps, completed) and \"Fix timeout\" (2 steps, completed), timestamped across May 2-5. No actual learnings are recorded. No mistakes logged.\n\nMeanwhile, the builder has been actively working (security issues fixed in Phase 1, 5 build phases completed, tender system migration implemented). The work happened; the learning didn't persist.\n\nBy contrast, effective experts show clear learning accumulation:\n- **crawler**: 6 learnings from one session (DDG rename, regex vs LLM, rate limiting, dedup, PETRONAS format, price bounds)\n- **reviewer**: 12 learnings across 3 review phases (SSRF, verification, state transitions)\n- **architect**: 4 learnings including the critical FTS5 column-position bug catch\n\n**Connection:**\n\nThe analyst's mistake log captures the exact meta-problem: \"Each step tried to do everything from scratch.\" Without persistent learnings, the builder reconstructs context every time. The conductor's learning to \"Track builder step count — if it exceeds expected steps, check if it's stuck or looping\" is downstream evidence of this — the builder may be looping because it doesn't remember what already worked.\n\nThe reviewer's learning \"Always verify builder's claims against actual code\" further suggests a trust gap that proper builder learnings would reduce.\n\n**Action items:**\n- [ ] **[HIGH]** Purge builder's corrupted learnings.md and initialize with actual learnings extracted from retro summaries (security fixes, timeout fix, build patterns)\n- [ ] **[MEDIUM]** Add a post-task hook: after each builder completion, auto-extract 1-3 learnings to learnings.md\n- [ ] **[LOW]** Audit all expert learnings.md files for staleness — most haven't been updated since March-April\n\n---\n\n### Insight 4: Scout and Crawler Have Identical Knowledge — 4th Report Flagging Unmerged Duplicates (MEDIUM)\n\n**Evidence:**\n\nSide-by-side comparison of scout and crawler learnings:\n\n| Learning | Crawler | Scout |\n|----------|---------|-------|\n| DDG package renamed to `ddgs` | Yes | Yes (identical) |\n| Regex > LLM for price extraction | \"58% hit rate on 332 items\" | \"58% hit rate on 332 items\" (identical) |\n| Rate limiting: SEARCH_DELAY = 1.5 | Yes | Yes (identical) |\n| Dedup before batch | Yes | Yes (identical) |\n| Price sanity bounds 10-5M MYR | Yes | Yes (identical) |\n| PETRONAS pricesheet format | Yes | No |\n| Multi-currency handling | No | Yes |\n| Query specificity strategy | No | Yes |\n\n5 of 7 crawler learnings are duplicated verbatim in scout. The analyst's learning about \"Keep it focused — one step, one action\" applies: merging two learnings files is a trivial task that keeps getting deprioritized against seemingly larger items.\n\n**Action items:**\n- [ ] **[MEDIUM]** Merge scout and crawler learnings NOW — consolidate into crawler, add scout-only items (multi-currency, query specificity), deduplicate\n- [ ] **[LOW]** Consider whether scout and crawler should be separate experts — their domains overlap almost entirely\n\n---\n\n### Insight 5: Infrastructure Regression After \"Peak Stability\" — rag-server 32 Restarts, harga 6 (MEDIUM)\n\n**Evidence:**\n\nW26 celebrated \"peak stability\" with 0 restarts across all 18 processes. Given the 5,331 new tenders requiring embedding, this may be index growth hitting the memory limit.\n\nembed-server dropped from 1,023MB to 202.9MB — the torchcodec fix is still holding, but the memory drop suggests it may have recently restarted and not fully loaded its model yet.\n\nocr-server grew from 2,085MB to 3.1GB — 50% memory growth with no obvious trigger.\n\n**Connection:**\n\nThe devops expert's known issue \"PM2 max_memory_restart env var leak\" could be at play again for rag-server.
